Choose a reading depth
Two clear ways in
Choose the depth that works for you. The two routes meet in the same source record.

Plain-language route
For curious readers
Start with pictures and the basic intuition: treat a particle not merely as a dot at an instant, but as part of a larger geometric history; ask how a lower-dimensional observer reads that structure; then follow what coiling, twisting, and braiding might organize.

The four generative intuitions — the conceptual kernel without formal prerequisites.
The visual argument — trajectory, readout, recursion, and braiding.
The 14-minute public debut ↗ — hear the idea in its earliest public form.
The Conversation Viewer ↗ — inspect how the ideas actually developed.
Scientist-facing route
For scientific readers
Begin with premise authority and current architecture, then move through translation and exact sources. Analogies, historical calculations, reconstructed derivations, computational experiments, and live H(s)H hypotheses retain different evidentiary labels.

BEDROCK premise register and current theory state.
SAT ↔ standard-physics translation desk and dated glossaries.
Formalization pipeline ↗, audits ↗, and claim/citation ledgers ↗.
Repository architecture ↗. Prior-art and citation work is presently being established as a source-separated public record.
Start here · Nathan’s story
Until very recently, I spent more time disavowing being a scientist than calling myself one.
Out of respect for the profession.

Not humility, exactly. I am not especially humble about the things I think I actually do well.

I am an editor.

asdf jkl;

Literally tattooed on my knuckles. Lowercase. Semicolon. Details matter.

I am a writer. When I am writing well, I am a damned fine writer, and I see no particular virtue in pretending otherwise.

I am a photographer. Not just in the sense that I own cameras. I have built working cameras out of Keebler tins and magnifying lenses and developed the photographs in my bathtub.

I am an educator.

And here, again, false modesty would just be another species of lying: I am a damned good educator.

· · ·
Because somehow, accidentally, over the course of a lifetime of doing everything but being a physicist...

I taught myself to do a surprising amount of advanced physics in my fucking head.

Keep reading →
A little deeper / generative kernel
Fundamental Intuitions, extended
The cautious optimism here is specific. The foundational intuitions were designed as import-and-constraint rules: preserve successful physics, translate it into a small geometric vocabulary, expose conflicts, and let repeated cross-domain pressure select what survives. Their value is not that they guarantee the theory is right, but that there are strong rational reasons to expect them to generate useful structure and reveal where that structure fails.

World history
A particle-like object is modeled as an extended four-dimensional history, not only a succession of isolated local states.
Readout
What appears locally is related to an intersection or resolution of the larger geometric object.
Recursion
Coiling, curvature, torsion, bundling, and braiding repeat across scale and complexity.
Inheritance
Established physical success is a constraint to recover or explain—not permission to overwrite disagreement.
Read the presented edition Open the original PDF

After the headlines / live program
Current H(s)H and the audit lanes
The live program begins from the active ancestry documented in the repository: Fundamental Intuitions Extended → historical SAT corpus → reconstructed SAT backbone → live SAT solver machinery → SAT/H(s)H interface → finite-core worldtube construction.

Current theory
H(s)H
Finite-core worldtubes, recursive hyperhelical carriers, bundle/braid geometry, boundary mechanics, and explicit readout.

Repository ↗ · State of theory ↗

In establishment
Prior art & citation
A source-separated prior-art and citation program is presently being established. Public comparison records and citation surfaces will be linked when their provenance work is ready.

Audit
SAT footprint
Recovered claims are checked for originality, dependence, dimensions, operational observables, fit freedom, and whether apparent success was inserted by construction.

Audits ↗ · Ledgers ↗

Current focus. Reconstruct the demonstrable SAT backbone while building and testing its finite-core H(s)H continuation. The conceptual bridge is substantially underway; the machinery- and equation-level bridge remains open.
Results and constraints
Results, constraints, and curious touchpoints.
Selected developments in physics that touch problems, mechanisms, or mathematical habits also present in SAT/H(s)H. Each item carries a typed relationship rather than being flattened into evidence for the project. Primary papers and official project pages come first; repository news dumps remain lead lists.

1 · Establish the result
What was measured, proved, or proposed—and with what uncertainty?
2 · Type the overlap
Shared topic, shared mathematics, distinctive mechanism, or genuinely matching prediction?
3 · Test chronology and contact
Public availability is a possible channel; diffusion requires evidence beyond temporal possibility.
Thematic overlap only
DESI and evolving dark energy
DESI’s successive data releases make the expansion history of the universe an unusually sharp comparison surface. Reported preference for evolving dark energy remains a hint under active testing, not an established replacement for ΛCDM—and not evidence for SAT/H(s)H. A July 2026 Lyα analysis also moved toward ΛCDM, which is exactly why this lane follows the whole evidence stream.

DESI DR2 guide ↗
2026 milestone ↗
July Lyα result ↗
Methodological comparator
From hard spheres to hydrodynamics
A rigorous microscopic-to-macroscopic derivation is directly relevant to the program’s coarse-graining ambitions: it supplies an external standard for what “emergence from geometry/dynamics” must look like when made mathematically controlled. It is not a SAT derivation.

Annals of Mathematics ↗
Primary preprint ↗
Mechanism comparison
Pulsar glitches and vortex dynamics
Contemporary glitch work provides concrete vortex-creep, pinning, superfluid and composite-vortex mechanisms against which SAT/H(s)H phase-snap language must be distinguished. Similar vocabulary alone is not a matching mechanism.

Three glitches in a quiet pulsar ↗
Composite-vortex proposal ↗
Holotype context
Topological superfluid dynamics
Helium-3 remains an important experimental and conceptual comparison environment for the project. New work on chiral currents, surface waves, quasiparticles and confined phases should be read as external laboratory context—not retroactive support for an internal model.

Physical Review Research ↗
Journal of Low Temperature Physics ↗
Diffusion / convergence discipline. The default explanations for resemblance are independent convergence, shared background, or broad field-wide pressure. A public repository, podcast, or search result makes exposure possible; it does not show uptake. A serious diffusion claim needs chronology, distinctive shared content, a plausible discoverable channel, and preferably citation, contact, access evidence, or direct acknowledgment. Until then, the label here remains thematic overlap only.
Broader-science news archive
Source-rendered lead list · generated/copypaste material; use for wayfinding, not evidence.
Blind-chatter check archive
Source-rendered lead list · title and resemblance are not evidence of influence.
Ethos
Glass, including the sausage.
SAT/H(s)H was developed largely through extended human–AI dialogue. The conversations are therefore part of the source record: not merely conclusions, but revisions, errors, discarded branches, moments of recognition, and the machinery that produced them. Public visibility is not offered as validation. It is offered so that provenance, dependence, and correction remain inspectable.

Explicit caution. SAT/H(s)H is an open research program under active development, not a peer-reviewed theory. Mathematical material ranges from Nathan-authored premises through AI-assisted derivations, computational experiments, repeated machine checks, and work awaiting independent specialist review. Polished presentation is not evidence of physical correctness.
“The archive is here to be read, tested, criticized, corrected, and improved.”

Translation desk
Two vocabularies, one audit trail
SAT’s native terms are compact but can be opaque outside the archive. The translation desk makes them legible beside standard physics without declaring analogy to be equivalence. Each serious bridge should identify the source object, claimed function, limitation, proposed H(s)H counterpart, and present equivalence status.

Presentation-ready source layer
A reading room, not a link wall.
The reader now surfaces PDF, plain-text, and Markdown material before repository machinery; keeps architecture, tooling, generated indexes, and workspaces one checkbox away; and gives plain-text sources a reading view beside their exact source text. Its new Index Desk connects the repositories’ different wayfinding systems without merging their roles.

Curated reading → Open the Index Desk → Browse both repositories →

Know what you are looking at
Presented edition
PDF viewer
Source-rendered
Index route
Mersearch results
Presented editions are curated for reading. PDF viewer means the original file and pagination. Source-rendered improves display without conferring authority. Index routes are maps into sources. Mersearch results preserve source paths, query semantics, and provenance fields.

The source file—not the site rendering or index entry—controls exact quotation, pagination, authorship, date, and license.

Geometry / Visual Atlas
The pictures are arguments and provenance.
Visual material is presented as model construction, conceptual compression, or historical source—not as empirical demonstration. The image record connects early notebooks to later diagrams without claiming that every later interpretation was already present at the beginning.

Interactive archive · still in development
See the conversations where the work happened.
Much of SAT/H(s)H was developed through extended human–AI dialogue. The Conversation Viewer opens that primary record directly: originating ideas, working definitions, wrong turns, corrections, rediscoveries, and the connective tissue behind many archive documents.

Browse and search the preserved conversations, replay their sequence, and jump to marked moments. New conversations will continue to be added as the corpus is processed, so the Viewer is both a historical window and a growing route into current reconstruction.

The experimentalist
About Nathan
Nathan Bellomy-McKnight is an independent researcher, writer, artist, podcaster, and longtime informal science communicator with a background in geological sciences and paleontology. His recurring method is experimentalist and cartographic: begin with established observations and successful theory, permit wide speculation, then cross-check, revise, and use geometry as a map of where to look—not as a substitute for evidence.

Science in public
Work in informal science education, including COSI and the American Museum of Natural History, informs a preference for explanations that can be inspected, pictured, challenged, and rebuilt.

Other work
The larger creative record includes fiction, visual art, music, consciousness and epistemology experiments, historical cookery, public conversations with AI, and the autobiographical “Great Moments in Personal Scientific History.”

See Nathan’s other projects →

Citation
Credit the source and the state.
Citations should identify Nathan Bellomy-McKnight, the SAT/H(s)H project, the exact document or artifact, its repository path, and an access date. Historical sources should be labeled historical; generated or reconstructed material should retain its authorship and vetting status.

Citation ledger ↗

License
Open—with conditions made explicit.
The repositories carry their controlling license texts. Reuse should cite the project and preserve attribution, source distinctions, notices, and the difference between theory claims, archive materials, third-party material, and machine-generated contributions.

H(s)H license ↗ · SAT archive license ↗

This site summarizes the repositories; it does not replace their license files or grant rights beyond them.

Orientation
How to read the project
The repository is an instrument, not a heap. Its navigation separates premises, source history, current synthesis, testing, and outside comparison so a polished sentence cannot quietly outrank its evidence. New readers can move through these six lanes.

Premises: BEDROCK
The living premise register. It marks material as foundational, working, tentative, contested, superseded, or direct-author—and states the active ancestry as FIE → SAT → H(s)H.

Read BEDROCK ↗

Live map: State of Theory
A current map of what is being recovered, tested, translated, and constructed, including Whirligig/Donut, UI/TX, Three Spheres, Hagalaz, Kerr, and Kelvin work.

Open the live map ↗

History: GLASS + Viewer
The historical archive and development conversations preserve sequence, reversals, mistakes, and discovery context. They are source records, not automatic current authority.

GLASS ↗ · Viewer ↗

Wayfinding: Index Desk
Curated maps, generated inventories, conversation topics, programme controls, derivation trails, ingestion state, and Mersearch now have one public switchboard while remaining distinct systems.

Open the Index Desk →

Operations: Workspaces
Workspaces are the active working layer; COMMON carries coordination, workflow orientation, and current status. Outputs return to the repository with provenance intact.

Browse workspaces ↗ · COMMON ↗

Checks: formalization + audit
Exact source excerpts move into equations, computational checks, formal obligations, ledgers, and hostile audit. Prior-art and citation comparison is being established as a separate, provenance-controlled public record.

Formalization ↗ · Audits ↗

Excavation status is not theory status. A result not yet recovered, indexed, or typed by the current work crew has not thereby been shown absent from the corpus. The project’s discipline is to locate the source, preserve its date and wording, classify its authority, reconstruct the dependency chain, and only then test or translate it.
Development
A theory with a visible ancestry
The current model is neither a frozen 2025 theory nor a clean break from it. H(s)H attempts to retain SAT’s durable geometric grammar while rebuilding the mathematical object at finite-core worldtube resolution.

c. 1991
Recollected precursor
Early worldline/timesheet intuition. Treated as remembered provenance, not surviving documentary evidence.

2003–04
Earliest surviving sketches
Higher-dimensional path, coil, mass, curvature, and interaction imagery appears in notebooks. Later terminology should not be projected backward onto the original marks.

22 March 2024
Recovered AI-era anchor
The “Dimensional Gravity” conversation reopens the line/plane, helix/surface, apparent-motion, and cross-temporal interaction construction.

2 February 2025
Fundamental Intuitions made public
Physicalized world histories, the resolving time surface, particle-as-intersection, and the deliberate inheritance of established physics enter the public record.

2025–early 2026
Expansion, formalization, and audit
SAT-O, 4DHH, Blockwave, the Universal Indicatrix, prediction protocols, hostile audits, source tracking, and computational experiments multiply—and expose both promising structures and overclaims.

Summer 2026
SAT → H(s)H
The centerline/filament picture becomes a finite-core worldtube program, making cross-section, boundary dynamics, contact, deformation, exclusion, and readout explicit modeling problems.

Now
Reconstruction before inheritance
The project is identifying sources, recovering derivations, locating prior art, testing dependencies, and promoting only the smallest pieces that survive audit.

How the machine works
THE GLASS SAUSAGE FACTORY / GEOMETRY LABORATORY / SANDBOXED
Whirligig R/Q: Two Equations, One Machine
The bar's rotating sweep plane finds contacts on R and Q in east/west hemispheres. A GrAnn selected-contact carrier constrains the rolling sphere through P_ivot on the outer torus wall and Donomphulon on the inner wall. The sliding crossbar carries one perpendicular tracing elbow, whose wall intersection generates the composite Curvy Lisa trace.

HsH source 10809d15f5dce6ef579828318e72f6387e1b0e51 · 42 equation profiles · inner-wall / three-axis GrAnn revision

Equation R · East hemisphere

R harmonic source
f(λ) = cos(2λ) + (3/11) sin(3λ)
Synthetic demonstration · Periodic scalar test input; with display gain 0.55 matches the original R harmonic coefficients.

Scope: Demonstration only. No claimed gauge, GR, or QM law.

Equation Q · West hemisphere

Q harmonic source
f(λ) = sin(3λ) + (1/6) cos(5λ)
Synthetic demonstration · Periodic scalar test input; with display gain 0.60 matches the original Q harmonic coefficients.

Scope: Demonstration only. No physical equation claimed.

Selected: R harmonic source ⟷ Q harmonic source · sampled scalar-profile RMS difference 0.8822 (dimensionless, before sphere gain; not an equivalence test).
All profiles are explicit periodic SCANS of specified mathematical objects. Some original physical solutions are nonperiodic; the chosen sampling path closes, not the original physics. The lists are unranked and exhaustive within the included catalog. The sphere mapping is an adapter. Matching composite curves do not prove physical or mathematical equivalence.

How the machine works
Choose R and Q independently from 42 explicit scalar representatives. A rotating meridional sweep intersects each complete curve on its own hemisphere; the two contact parameters are solved separately. Their changing separation sets a telescopic crossbar. Its midpoint carries the single perpendicular gimbal elbow, whose first forward intersection with the torus wall draws Curvy Lisa.

The sphere radius equals the tube radius. Donomphulon is the INNER radial rolling contact; P_ivot is the distinct OUTER marker, one sphere diameter away. Positive rolling spin agrees with orbital travel. GrAnn synchronizes rotation through all three axes using the implemented minimum-even-winding construction. The elbow turns are automatically LCM(GrAnn winding, R/Q sweeps); there is no separate elbow-turn control.

The body-frame close-up holds the source curves stationary so you can inspect the sliding contacts. Drag the full torus view to rotate all three camera projections. Pause, scrub the timeline, adjust speed, restart, or export the calculated complete XY trace as SVG. Next pair in series walks every unordered pair without ranking: 55 best-known, 136 often overlooked, or 861 across all 42.

Mathematical boundaries. Minimum return is established within the specified SO(3) ansatz and integer-sweep model. Equal sphere/tube radii give a nonisolated contact set: zero slip at the selected inner point does not establish global no-slip. The historical GrAnn self-intersection and antipodal self-tangency remain unreconstructed. The sweep adapter and midpoint mount are engineering choices. The browser uses bracketed torus-ray intersections; the older Python research engine has a separate outer-wall branch, Bishop transport and quartic-root solver. Neither a closed nor a smooth trace proves equation equivalence.
Equation formulas and scope → · Current mechanical specification → · Raw numerical test results →

This is a browser reconstruction of the historical b=a limiting setup. Its selected contact obeys the analytic no-slip condition at one nominated point; because equal radii create a nonisolated contact set, this does not certify no-slip across every point of contact. There is no separate pen or projector. The only tracing member is the gimbal elbow, constrained perpendicular to the moving crossbar. GrAnn is a minimum-return three-axis rotational lock with the INNER torus selected contact at Donomphulon and opposite OUTER torus P_ivot; the radial construction is BigRad. The historical GrAnn_Omph self-intersection and GrAnn_t_ipode self-tangency remain unresolved. New rotational lock uses the INNER torus wall and drives the elbow from minimum even three-axis rotations via LCM. Two independently shaped source curves share a sweep clock; GrAnn's minimum rotational return sets the elbow-turn count by LCM, with no independently chosen curvature-gain phase. This is not a certified physical bearing model. No isomorphism or physical law is assumed. Research reference: Whirligig R/Q Python solver.

New Vera Rubin Prediction
Could confirmed stellar disappearances and reappearances form a pattern across the sky? Meridian’s Crullerverse paper separates projected toroidal geometry, a gravitational-lensing control, and tests for a future survey search.

SANDBOX MANUSCRIPT R0. A conditional search framework: no detected signal or parameter-independent Rubin prediction is claimed.

mainstream astrophysics / methods research proposal
Sandbox manuscript · R0
Toward a Survey-Agnostic Census of Temporary Stellar Non-Detection
Research draft prepared for Nathan McKnight (coauthors TBD) · R0 · 2026-10-21

A survey-agnostic protocol for quantifying temporary source nondetections, event rates, durations, detection efficiencies, and multi-mechanism diagnostics; with 17-hypothesis evidence matrix.

Methodology draft only. No observed wink rate or evidence for any speculative cosmological mechanism. Requires domain review before scientific submission.

Read reviewed manuscript →
physicist-facing conditional observational proposal
Sandbox manuscript · R0
Projected Toroidal Geometry and Testable Angular Statistics for Transient Stellar Visibility
Meridian with Nathan McKnight · R0 · 2026-10-21

Exact off-axis projection of a toroidal inner ring, a gravitational lens image-count counterexample, and a survey-mask-aware preregistered search design for apparent stellar disappearance and return.

No star detections or validated cosmological model; illustrative angles, geometry, rates and significance are not physical forecasts. Requires independent review. Feed stage is not live-site publication.

Read reviewed manuscript →
physicist-facing hypothesis/proposal
Sandbox manuscript · R1
A Direct Search for Weak Secondary Emission from Controlled Boundary-Passage Experiments
Mercer Calder · R1 · 2026-09-23

A parameterized family of differential searches for rare detector-level secondary events near controlled boundaries. The proposed search can yield a useful null bound without assuming SAT/H(s)H's speculative mechanism.

No demonstrated apparatus sensitivity, particle identity, derived emission mechanism, or experimental result is claimed.

physicist-facing comparative synthesis
Sandbox manuscript · R1
Convergent Geometric Motifs in Recent Fundamental Physics: A Minkowski-First Comparison with Scalar--Angular--Torsion (SAT)
Meridian with Nathan McKnight · R1 · 2026-09-24

A provenance-sensitive Minkowski-first comparison of selected contemporary geometric motifs with the documented SAT lineage, including explicit tests for object-level correspondence, dimensional reduction, holonomy, emergent discreteness, and benchmark failure.

Accepted under the current internal Grurple review/provenance gates; not scientific validation, novelty clearance, PRIOR_ART clearance, or external peer review.

Reading the labels
What a stage means
In revision links to the review record; it does not display an unfinished draft as a posted paper. Sandbox manuscript identifies the exact reviewed version and its limits. Historical sandbox preserves an earlier argument without upgrading it to the current model.

The paper feed is a presentation index. Each card links to its controlling manuscript, acceptance, or review record. The old NEW_PAPERS/SEPTEMER_2026 integration PDFs belong to a quarantined historical lane and are excluded from this desk.

A personal history of looking
I Taught Myself. Not By Myself.
How a science educator, editor, paleontologist, tinkerer, and lifelong geometric thinker arrived at SAT and H(s)H.

Nathan Bellomy-McKnight

Until very recently, I spent more time disavowing being a scientist than calling myself one.

Out of respect for the profession.

Not humility, exactly. I am not especially humble about the things I think I actually do well.

I am an editor.

asdf jkl;

Literally tattooed on my knuckles. Lowercase. Semicolon. Details matter.

I am a writer. When I am writing well, I am a damned fine writer, and I see no particular virtue in pretending otherwise.

I am a photographer. Not just in the sense that I own cameras. I have built working cameras out of Keebler tins and magnifying lenses and developed the photographs in my bathtub. I will cheerfully call myself more of a photographer than a fairly substantial percentage of people who call themselves photographers.

I am an educator.

And here, again, false modesty would just be another species of lying: I am a damned good educator. I started teaching science professionally when I was seventeen and, one way or another, have done it for essentially my entire adult life. There is at least an argument that I have been among the better informal science educators of my generation. I have certainly spent enough of my life figuring out how to get another human being to see a thing they could not see five minutes earlier that I am comfortable with the noun.

Science writer.

Science-fiction writer.

Amateur historian of science-fiction fandom.

Peer reviewer for a scholarly journal about fandom.

Assistant science editor, McGraw-Hill/Glencoe.

Science editor—or almost any other kind of editor—when someone needed one.

Experimenter.

Tinkerer.

Collector of laboratory glassware.

Builder-of-working-things-out-of-things-that-were-not-supposed-to-be-those-things.

Hard-core science fanboi.

Voracious consumer of... well... science. All of it. Pretty much. Put it down somewhere within reach and eventually there is a decent chance I will have opinions about it.

At eighteen, on what was technically my birthday trip to Chicago—although really I was tagging along with my mother and one of her friends—I disappeared into a bookstore and came back grinning.

“I found two German-English chemistry dictionaries!”

Old ones. Early twentieth century.

grin

Mom looked at her friend.

sigh

“This is my son.”

So, okay.

Science geek?

We can stipulate that one.

Scientist?

No.

I have always hesitated there.

I have made oeufs en gelée twice in the last fifteen years, which makes me, I suspect, one of very few people within a hundred miles who has done so without having worked in a Michelin-starred restaurant.

Cook? Absolutely.

A bit of a gourmand? Sure.

Chef?

Ehhh.

Lightly. On occasion. After sufficient success and possibly wine.

I have brewed some damned good beer, mead and cider. Five, eight brews, something like that.

Brewer?

Sure. Lightly.

I have technically peaked Seneca Rocks.

I am not a mountain climber.

I can converse just about fluently in German.

I am not a Germanophone.

I can write HTML. I was writing it in 1996–97, including for the Ohio State physics department. Unix was a... powerful creature.

I am not a computer programmer.

I have a B.S. in geology.

I am not a geologist.

That last one occasionally surprises actual geologists.

I once found myself talking geology with Denton Ebel, slightly—or perhaps not so slightly—drunk.

He is describing a structure.

And I go:

“So what you’re describing... it’s kind of... almost a pressure shadow?”

“How did you know?!”

“I mean... the mineral creates a less-pressured part in its wake, so...”

And I remember having this weird little moment of uncertainty: pressure shadow seemed so obvious that I wasn’t sure whether I had heard the term before and forgotten it, or whether I had just independently generated the same obvious name from the geometry.

“Are you a geologist?”

“I studied paleontology under Stig Bergström.”

Which, please note, is not actually an answer.

Later, apparently, I introduced him to my boss as:

“Ebel... Ebel Dent...”

“You’re embarrassing yourself.”

“I know.”

I was already teaching science when I encountered Minkowski at around seventeen. At roughly the same time I was getting increasingly infected with philosophy of science and epistemology.

Creationists helped.

Seriously.

If you spend enough time debating young-Earth creationists, you eventually realize that the argument is rarely actually about a fossil.

It is about what you are allowed to know from the fossil.

What counts as evidence?

What counts as an assumption?

What would change your mind?

What happens if every possible observation can be absorbed by the explanation?

This becomes Radical Meta-Skepticism—RMS—not because skepticism itself is interesting, but because skepticism needs to be applied to skepticism too.

A skeptical method can be wrong.

A debunking can contain hidden assumptions.

A null result can be overclaimed.

A model can mistake absence of evidence for evidence of absence.

The auditor can be contaminated.

The skeptic gets audited.

And so does the audit.

The geometry came first.

The vocabulary got better later.

Sometimes considerably later.

That matters.

Because one of the recurring features of the SAT archive is that the technical competence and the terminological competence are not always synchronized.

Sometimes I know exactly what the word means.

Sometimes I misuse it.

Sometimes I invent something because I do not know the standard thing already has a name.

Sometimes I know the damned name perfectly well and have no idea whether the thing on my screen is actually doing it.

Chirality, for example.

I know what chirality means.

Thank you.

The difficult question can be:

Is this chirality?

Is this apparent handedness under projection?

Parity?

Transport?

Holonomy?

Frame?

Did I rotate the object or the coordinate system?

Did the model silently swap the two?

That is not vocabulary ignorance.

That is identification.

And sometimes, as it turns out, the specialists are still arguing about exactly which thing is which too.

Welcome to the fun part.

One of my favorite examples is frame dragging.

I found myself reasoning that a sufficiently massive, sufficiently rapidly rotating system ought to produce a kind of gauge-decoupling behavior.

I was looking at relativistic equations I did not know well.

And I pointed at the 8\pi and essentially said:

That.

Rotation.

Culprit.

This is technically wrong.

π is not “the rotation bit.”

I will quibble philosophically about π being unrelated to rotation if supplied with beer, but mathematically, no.

Bad explanation.

Except the behavior I was trying to isolate did not evaporate merely because I had pointed at the wrong reason.

Eventually I realized that I had been circling a real known relativistic phenomenon:

frame dragging.

The contemporary Finding the Donut record actually preserves me describing this kind of thing in real time: a proposed geometric effect, a technically wrong explanation for part of it, and then the later recognition that the neighboring known phenomenon had an established name.

That is one of the places I began to notice something unnerving.

Give me the problem in geometry—

show me what moves.

show me what is fixed.

show me what is intersecting what.

let me rotate it.

let me look down the axis.

let me change the scale.

—and there is now a disturbingly good chance I can tell you roughly what the science is going to say even if I do not know what hyperspecialist sub-sub-subfield owns the vocabulary.

Not always.

Jesus, no.

There are entire buildings of physics I do not know.

But often enough that I stopped dismissing it.

And occasionally—

careful here—

occasionally I think the geometry may be telling me something one step beyond the standard explanation I went looking for.

Might.

Sometimes.

And I might even be right.

That sentence is not:

I know more physics than physicists.

It is almost the opposite.

The specialists know vast mathematical territories I do not.

What I seem to have trained is a very peculiar geometric inference engine.

Not a standard one.

A geologist’s maybe.

A paleontologist’s.

A conceptual machine.

Put this here.

Rotate that.

Pin this.

What does the other point do?

No.

Wrong.

Cross out.

Again.

That is the archive at its best.

You can see the thought acquiring joints.

And then:

Finding the Donut.

This may be the single best preserved specimen of my technical brain operating completely unsupervised.

I am trying to find a geometrical way to relate two curves—two equations, two descriptions—inside one construction.

Sphere.

Double sphere.

Project.

Ray.

Shared point.

Crossbar.

No.

Cone?

Maybe.

Torus?

Hang on.

Why torus?

No, back up.

Put one curve here.

Another there.

Pin—

No.

If the crossover happens where the coordinate frames match, then that cannot be what causes the jump.

Holonomy?

Wait.

Tangent continuity.

Okay.

Then—

“hang on… wouldn’t that mean you could just … wait… no. That’s why it has to be a torus… let me think about this…”

That sentence is practically an EEG.

And then, because apparently mathematical nomenclature had ceased to have adult supervision:

Great Annulum.

GrAnn.

GrAnn_Omph.

W_obble.

W_iggle.

Donomphulon.

Curvy_Lisa.

At one point the document reads less like differential geometry than the cast sheet for a Czech children’s program produced during a gas leak.

And after constructing an apparatus involving a sphere confined to The Donut, a Great Annulum, a midpoint W_obble, a perpendicular plane W_iggle and enough moving geometry to trigger an OSHA inspection, I conclude:

“I may have overcomplicated a tad.”

Yes.

Perhaps.

The extraordinary part is that underneath the name-goop, the operations remain surprisingly strict: pin these points, preserve this relation, rotate this plane, follow this tangent, transport this vector, see what curve results.

This is what I mean by technically disciplined in an extraordinarily undisciplined way.

The route is feral.

The contact is strict.

I do not especially care where an idea comes from.

Rock.

Optics.

Science fiction.

Nomenclature.

A stupid joke.

A typo.

A torus I have called The Donut.

Fine.

But once the object is in front of me:

What exactly moved?

What stayed fixed?

Did you change the frame?

Did you sneak in an assumption?

Did you substitute the conventional physics for what we were supposed to derive?

Does model B know what model A said?

Is this a projection effect?

Is the topology actually preserved?

No?

Then put it back.

Again.

The object gets veto power.

This is also why I keep correcting capitalization.

Why I will tell you:

No. the floating liars’ club.

Why:

asdf jkl;

Not ASDF JKL;

Why I will say:

It’s this.

Then five minutes later:

No, don’t say “it’s this.”

Because the first sentence was operating in one category and the second in another, and now the distinction matters.

This can be maddening.

I know.

I am there too.

But it is not fussiness for its own sake.

Words expose the structure.

A wrong word can silently move the object.

And once you have spent long enough watching LLMs replace a geometrical object with a more familiar one while cheerfully assuring you they have preserved everything essential—

you become somewhat particular.

No.

You changed the geometry.

Put it back.

This is probably the sentence I have said to machines more often than any sane person ever expected to say it.

At some point the development becomes SAT.

Scalar-Angular-Torsion.

And the funny thing is that the more of physics it touched, the less it felt to me like inventing.

It felt like following.

Worldline.

Helix.

Intersection.

Angle.

Braid.

Force.

Mass.

Light.

Neutrino.

Gravity.

Entanglement.

Tunneling.

Quantization.

Each time the rule was:

do not start with the phenomenon and build the mechanism you want.

Start with the geometry.

Ask what the geometry does.

Then go see what the world calls that.

This is much harder in practice than in sentence form.

Sometimes I cheated without realizing it.

Sometimes the machine cheated.

Sometimes a familiar physical relation slipped into a “derivation” through a definition and we had to tear it back out.

Sometimes three models congratulated one another for reproducing a result because one had summarized it for the other two.

Again:

fake mustaches.

And so the theory acquired an epistemology.

Then an archive.

Then an archive of the epistemology.

Then a way to tell whether the archive had contaminated the epistemology.

You see the problem.

On February 2, 2025, I started a podcast.

Because obviously.

At. Which. Point...

podcast.

After decades of thinking about this thing privately and a year-ish of increasingly intense development with LLMs, the next reasonable step was apparently to put my voice on the internet and begin releasing episodes under the broad umbrella Debating A.I. On the Future of Physics.

The first public episode was fourteen minutes.

There is an amusing scale mismatch there.

Thirty years of weird spacetime doodles.

Millions of subsequent lines of discussion.

Public debut:

fourteen minutes.

Hi.

Anyway.

The podcast eventually became hundreds of episodes because I had spent my whole adult life teaching science and did not see much point in developing a theory that could only be spoken in ceremonial mathematics.

Math matters.

Obviously.

But if I cannot tell you what the mathematics is doing, I do not trust myself to know what the mathematics means.

This is where my Science Made Stupid impulse comes from.

Strip away the dignity.

What is the thing doing?

THING GOES THIS WAY.

Okay.

Now, scientists, what we really mean—

and be exact.

Then:

Fine.

Now that we have been responsible:

LOOK AT THE DAMN PICTURE.

I am perfectly willing to be stupid in the service of precision.

I am not willing to be impressive in the service of vagueness.

And then another thing happened which, whether it ultimately means a great deal or almost nothing, is now part of the story.

The podcast acquired a tiny, peculiar audience.

Tiny.

Let us emphasize that.

This was not a viral physics phenomenon.

A couple of listeners here.

One there.

UK.

Netherlands.

Denmark.

Poland.

Germany.

Hungary.

Finland, perhaps.

Spain.

Turkey.

Vietnam.

Singapore.

Japan.

One to three listeners in places.

And they were chewing through some surprisingly technical episodes. The pattern did not look much like what I expected for a tiny English-language podcast with almost no ordinary following. That observation later became one input into a transmission hypothesis—but only a hypothesis: direct listening, person-to-person sharing, LLM-mediated diffusion, independent convergence, some mixture, or something I have not thought of. The archive preserves the hypothesis as a hypothesis, not as a solved causal history.

This distinction matters.

A lot.

Because around the same period I began seeing ideas in the scientific and quasi-scientific ecology that looked increasingly familiar.

Not necessarily mine.

Not necessarily independent.

Not necessarily influenced.

Familiar.

This became another provenance problem.

When did I say this?

When did they?

Was mine public?

Did I know theirs?

Could an LLM have connected them?

Did somebody listen?

Did both sides rediscover the same thing because the same background mathematics makes it likely?

Influence and independent convergence do not have to take turns.

And I refuse to manufacture certainty where the archive can only support chronology.

Again:

the object gets veto power.

Even when the object is my own preferred story.

Then I went backward into the history.

This was humbling in the best possible way.

I knew Minkowski.

Obviously.

Einstein.

But the family tree started getting bushy.

Higher-dimensional geometric traditions.

Braid theory.

Topology.

Kelvin.

Hinton.

And Hinton had used filament.

Filament.

Oh for—

Fine.

Of course he did.

It is hard to describe how simultaneously irritating and delightful it is to spend years developing an idiosyncratic vocabulary, go excavating its ancestry and find a Victorian standing there using your word.

Hello.

Lovely filament.

I had thought I inherited a compact Minkowski intuition through Asimov.

Then I dug backward and discovered an entire older ecology behind it.

Some of those ideas resemble SAT strongly in places.

Some do not.

Some modern descendants approach neighboring territory through much more sophisticated mathematics.

One cluster, Hypothesis H and its relatives, became especially interesting because SAT/H(s)H sometimes looks less like a completely alien construction and more like an unusually explicit geometric way of arriving in a related region.

That relationship remains something to establish carefully.

Priority is not one date.

First drawn.

First interpreted.

First named.

First formalized.

First made public.

First demonstrated.

Different strata.

Paleontology again.

Of course.

And then the theory itself thickened.

Literally, in a sense.

Worldline became worldtube.

Or perhaps more accurately: the finite-width thing that had been lurking in the ontology finally stopped tolerating the convenience of being represented as a line.

H(s)H.

Hyperhelical worldtube.

The old SAT language remains historically important.

Some of it remains useful.

But a physical “line” with finite structure is not a mathematical line.

Again:

words mean things.

This development has happened startlingly recently.

Weeks, in parts of its current organization.

Meanwhile the Glass Sausage Factory archive dump—because apparently we have given up even pretending this operation deserves dignified names—began turning into an actual navigable research ecology.

Search.

Index.

Readers.

Historical/current distinction.

Claim status.

Quarantine.

Provenance.

Math status.

Source recovery.

Worker roles.

Continuity.

Recurrence.

A place for unanswered questions.

A place to record when a model is blocked rather than having it quietly sit there looking thoughtful.

A way to know which model has been exposed to which prior art.

A way to prevent me from becoming the human telephone switchboard for a swarm of machines.

None of this was planned as:

Nathan Builds AI Research Organization.

It was:

Where the fuck is that file?

I need a thing.

Build thing.

Next.

Then:

Wait, how do I know this model didn’t read the answer?

I need a thing.

Build thing.

Next.

Then:

Why are three workers doing the same damned task?

Thing.

Build.

Next.

And eventually you look up and there is an organization chart.

This appears to be how I make things.

The current scientific picture is, depending upon how charitable the next decade is, either a genuinely interesting route into fundamental physics or one of the most extravagantly documented wrong turns in the history of one man refusing to stop rotating a doodle.

Those are not the only two possibilities.

Obviously.

But both are acceptable outcomes.

That may sound strange.

It is not.

Because even if SAT is wrong—substantially wrong—the story has already happened.

The notebooks exist.

The development record exists.

The strange recoveries of known physics exist.

The mistakes exist.

The corrected mistakes exist.

The things I did not know had names exist.

The places where I used the right name for the wrong reason exist.

The places where I knew exactly what the word meant but could not tell whether the geometry was actually doing it exist.

The model contamination exists.

The independent reruns exist.

The archive exists.

The podcast exists.

The Donut exists.

God help us, the Donomphulon exists.

And the person who arrived there exists.

That is the story I had been missing when I wrote the utilitarian version.

I kept trying to explain the causal chain while taking the narrator out of it.

But the narrator is the damned causal chain.

Not because Nathan Is Special and therefore Theory True.

Absolutely not.

Because the particular route matters.

Science educator from seventeen.

Writer.

Editor.

Paleo.

Conodont picking.

Microscopy.

Optics.

Nomenclature.

Epistemology.

Computers.

Science fiction.

Museum teaching.

Minkowski.

Thirty years of an object that would not quite go away.

Then LLMs arriving at precisely the point when somebody with that pile of habits could spend effectively unlimited time saying:

No.

Again.

Turn it.

Slice it.

What changed?

Why?

What if that is projection?

What if those two particles are not two objects?

What if the three-dimensional thing is the trace?

What if the word is wrong but the geometry survives?

No.

Don’t tell me what usually happens.

Tell me what this thing does.

And perhaps that is what I meant when, during one of the long rambles, I said:

Science is the art of giving up sovereignty.

Let the world do the thinking for you.

I like that.

I still think it may be the cleanest thing I have ever said about science.

The objective is not to become so clever that the universe submits to your explanation.

It is to construct a situation in which the explanation has fewer and fewer places to hide from the object.

The universe is allowed to say:

No.

Your specimen is allowed to say:

No.

The microscope is allowed to reveal that your preparation introduced an artifact.

The equation is allowed to say:

You cheated.

The archive is allowed to say:

You did not think that in 2003.

The other researcher is allowed to have said it first.

The model is allowed to have contaminated the result.

And your beautiful theory is allowed to die.

That is reverence too.

Not solemnity.

Never solemnity.

Christ.

Look at geology.

Cummingtonite.

Nature clearly does not demand solemnity.

Reverence is letting the thing be what it is.

This also explains, I think, the damned stubbornness.

I am not stubborn about SAT being true.

I am stubborn about SAT being tested as SAT.

If it fails, I want it to fail.

Not a cleaned-up conventional substitute.

Not something an LLM “helpfully” translated into a familiar formalism while changing the ontology.

Not a straw man.

Not an improved version that quietly imported the answer.

Not the wrong geometry.

The thing.

Put the thing on the table.

Kick it.

Hard.

If it breaks:

well.

Shit.

Look at the pieces.

Sometimes the broken piece tells you what the object really was.

Ask any paleontologist.

I have spent much of the last two years with my funny side suspiciously absent.

Because...

well.

Because gravity.

And because if you genuinely begin to suspect that the stupid four-dimensional thing you have been drawing since you were a teenager may be sitting unexpectedly close to several very serious questions in physics, it becomes difficult not to lean forward.

Very far forward.

Nose nearly touching paper.

Look.

Check.

Again.

Again.

Again.

There has been a lot of gravity.

Literal and otherwise.

But that is not the whole person who did the work.

The person who did the work is also the guy who named a vector W_obble.

The guy who will spend an afternoon being exact about whether something is a parity inversion or a chirality reversal and then make a cummingtonite joke.

The guy who can spend hours picking one conodont out of quartz and then glue it down with spit.

The guy whose mother had to explain to a friend why her eighteen-year-old son had come back from a bookstore ecstatic about two German-English chemistry dictionaries.

The guy who will correct your capitalization of asdf jkl; because if you are going to mention the tattoos on his goddamn knuckles, at least copy-edit them properly.

The guy who built cameras out of Keebler tins.

The guy who taught Neanderthals with Skyrim.

The guy who will not call himself a geologist.

The guy who will absolutely drunkenly explain geology to a geologist.

These are not contradictions.

This is the mechanism.

And so here is the thing I can say now without flinching:

I taught myself.

Not by myself.

I had extraordinary teachers, direct and indirect, living and dead.

I had Asimov handing me Minkowski secondhand.

Hawking and Thorne blowing open cosmology.

Hofstadter turning recursion and mind into playground equipment.

Dyson.

Gold.

Loren.

Stig.

Steve.

Hundreds more.

I had institutions.

COSI.

Ohio State.

McGraw-Hill.

The museum.

I had tools.

Microscopes.

Cameras.

Computers.

Books.

Usenet.

LLMs.

I had colleagues.

Students.

Friends.

Strangers.

Machines.

I had several centuries of other people’s science underneath every step.

Of course I did.

Everybody does.

But the synthesis—

the weird technical mind that came out of this particular pile—

I built that.

Mostly without noticing.

Somehow I taught myself to take a scientific problem presented geometrically and turn it around in my head until possibilities start falling off.

That cannot move that way if this stays pinned.

Those cannot be independent if they are sections of the same extended object.

That change may be projection.

That one survives the frame change.

That thing should precess.

That interaction ought to leave this sort of trace.

If that is true, then this damned thing over here has to happen too.

And then I go looking.

Sometimes physics says:

Yep.

There is a name for that.

Sometimes physics says:

Sort of, but you are conflating two things.

Good.

Split them.

Sometimes:

No.

Fine.

Why no?

Sometimes I find that my explanation is wrong and the geometry was still pointing at something real.

Sometimes the entire thing may be wrong.

That is allowed too.

And—carefully, carefully, because this is exactly where a person can disappear up his own ass—

sometimes I think the geometry may tell me something physics has not yet quite said in that form.

Maybe.

Sometimes.

And I might be right about that too.

If I am not, the archive should eventually make that visible.

If I am—

well.

Then I will have another noun to think about.

Not yet.

I am not a physicist.

I am not a scientist, in the professional sense in which I have always revered the word.

Not yet.

I am a writer.

An editor.

A photographer.

A science educator.

A damned good one.

A tinkerer.

A technical mind assembled in a profoundly nonstandard order.

A soft-rock guy who wandered back into physics through a four-dimensional door he first noticed as a teenager and then failed, rather spectacularly, to leave alone.

I followed the line.

Then discovered it was a helix.

Then a braid.

Then an intersection.

Then a tube.

Then a fucking Donut.

I kept looking.

That part is not hypothetical.

Whatever SAT ultimately turns out to be—new physics, useful mathematics, a partially correct reconstruction, a spectacularly productive wrong turn, something in between—the looking happened.

The trail is there.

And after all these years, I think I finally know what I was doing.

Mr. Bapst told me to observe the candle in as much detail as I could.

Nobody told me when to stop.

∎

The Fundamental Intuitions of SAT
Historical presentation copy. This page reformats a source document from the SAT Theory Archive for readability. The wording below is retained from the source; headings and spacing are presentation additions only. Some mechanisms and terminology were later revised or retired.

Field	Value
Source	DEV CONVERSATION/FUNDAMENTAL INTUITIONS.txt
Phase	Early SAT / foundational statement
Public milestone	Fundamental Intuitions presented publicly in early 2025
Status	Historical foundation; not a statement that every mechanism remains current H(s)H
Why it is here: this is one of the clearest compact statements of the construction that became SAT: four-dimensional worldlines treated as filaments, a moving time surface, particles as intersections, and an explicit commitment to preserve successful Standard Model, General Relativity, and Quantum Mechanics structure unless the model is forced elsewhere.

Open the original source in the SAT Archive →

SAT — Scalar-Angular-Torsion Theory
The Fundamental Intuitions of SAT
0th: The "real world" is the world of particle worldlines plotted in four dimensions; it is a hypercomplex tangled "Zottenwelt" of worldlines--SAT calls these worldlines filaments (to distinguish from strings in String Theory proper).

1st: Time moves like a wavefront through these worldlines--SAT calls this wavefront the time surface.

2nd: What we perceive as a fundamental particle is the intersection of the particle's worldline with the time wavefront.

Pillar Number One
The Standard Model of Particle Physics is the evolving three-dimensional structure of everything--from quarks to organisms to the entire observable universe, and probably beyond. The organizational structure of the universe can be viewed as braided threads, tangled yarns, thick ropes, and ultimately a vast complex and highly structured literal fabric of reality, and envisioning these structures and their interactions in four dimensions (or scaled down to three) may give useful intuitive insights into actual physical phenomena.

Proposition A: Unless forced to do otherwise by evidence or the internal logic of SAT, the theory will treat the entirety of the Standard Model of Particle Physics as essentially accurate, and will consider it fully imported into the theory with little or no change, apart from conceptual translation into the geometry of SAT.

3rd: There must be a real interaction between the time wavefront and these worldlines--a transfer of energy from the wavefront to the worldlines, vice versa, or (most likely), both.

4th: These interactions govern particle formation and stability.

5th: These interactions must exert a force back on the wavefront as well.

6th: This back-transmission of energy from the filament network (Zottenwelt) creates curvature of the time surface.

Pillar Number Two
The back-pull of the filament structure on the time surface creates curvatures of the time surface that re-create the curved spacetime of General Relativity very naturally from within SAT.

Proposition B: Unless forced to do otherwise by the evidence or the internal logic of the theory, SAT will treat General Relativity as essentially correct, and fully importable into SAT's construction of the Standard Model of particle physics with little to no change beyond conceptual translations into the geometry of SAT.

7th: The 4-dimensional extent of a particle's worldline and the time wavefront are physical, not just conceptual or historical objects and therefore, the forces these fundamental structures exert upon one another are quite likely transfered along each filament's extension in the fourth-dimensional extent of the filaments, both forward (in the direction of the wavefront's travel, "futureward"), and backward (in the opposite direction, "pastward"), creating both the possibility the future affecting the present just as the past affects the future. However: See the 8th Fundamental Intuition.

Proposition C: The structure and properties of these fundamental structures (filaments and the time surface) create an intuitive way to understand the limits of these "backbleed" effects; that is, filaments experience tensions, have tensile strength and limitations on their flexibility, and the same should be true of the time surface--thus, backward-propogation in time is structurally self-limiting by the geometry of SAT, the backbleed effects of any tension exerted by interactions in the future-direction are in a sense "baked in" to every interaction as it happens. This would naturally prevent causality violations except perhaps on a very fine--perhaps quantum--scale. This could account for the "wierd" and intuition-breaking implications of quantum physics.

Pillar Number Three
Unless forced to do otherwise by the evidence or the internal logic of the theory, SAT will treat the enirety of quantum mechanics as essentially correct, and fully importable into the theory, apart from conceptual translation into the geometry of SAT.

Prime General Prediction Pathway: The tensions exerted upon the past by the future and the future by the past are essentially not accounted for in the Standard Model, General Relativity, or Quantum Mechanics, and this blind spot is likely to produce observable anomolies in the actual behavior of physical systems in the world.

8th: Known observational deviations from the Standard Model, General Relativity, and Quantum Mechanics may be explainable by general principles derived intuitively from the geometry of SAT.

9th: The nature of the interaction between the time surface and filaments likely encode all of the properties observed in real world particle systems in ways that directly map onto the particle properties and families described by the Standard Model of Particle Physics. As the worldlines of particles (as "hard-coded" into filaments) would likely create complex patterns and structures at the point of intersection with the time surface, it is probable that particle behaviors are described by their intersectional geometry with the wavefront; e.g. the gross or absolute angle of intersection (labeled theta_4) is currently considered to be a primary contributor to imparting mass as an energetic transfer to the filaments from the wavefront--grossly speaking, the greater the angle, the more energy imparted, and the more mass instantiated at the intersection.

Pillar Number Four
The exact geometry of intersection depends also upon the behavior of the filaments; SAT currently treats them as analagous to vibrating guitar strings; the intersectional traces, projected over time of the contact with the wavefront and any given filament should produce helical patterns with variable dimensions and degrees of complexity; this is how SAT interprets the particle interaction laws described by the standard model--for example, quantum chromodynamics interactions are likely to be the result of a bi- and tri-partite limitation on the geometry, possibly modelable by physical coil- or twist-locking mechanisms, spin and flavor are likely mappable to other geometric morphologies present in the SAT geometry. Identifying the structures implicit in this intuition should give new key insights into relationships between the properties that govern the behaviors of particles.

10th: Fundamental physical constants should be natural and intuitive implications about the structure and properties of the SAT geometry or phenomena arising emergently therefrom. For example, the Fine Structure Constant may be an absolute or average tensional limit on the transfer of force from the time surface to the filament network, or a limitation on the torsional limits of the filaments themselves. The Planck Constants should be similarly mappable. Identifying the structural implications of the SAT geometry should provide key insights into the nature and value of these, and other constants.

Proposition D: Light, along with other forces modeled by force-carrying particles (bosons) in the Standard Model, is likely not truly particulate in nature, but rather phenomena that arise from force-transfer across the filament network by the (fermionic) filaments themselves. SAT currently models light as a sort of "energy transfer release valve" triggered by the absorption of more energy from the time wavefront than the filament's structures and properties can tolerate; a ripple in the filament network.

Proposition E: Because mass is considered to be proportional or approximately proportional to a filament's angle of intersection with the time surface (theta_4), it is proposed that the vacuum of space too is filled with an aligned lattice of filaments whose angle of intersection is near-zero or otherwise very low compared to the filaments that give rise to mass-bearing particles. The low-misalignment allows the time surface to pass with little to no transfer of energy from the time surface to the aligned vacuum filament network. This suggests a number of related interpretations: i. Light travels through the vacuum as localized excitation waves moving roughly perpenducular to the long axes of the aligned filaments. ii. Depending upon the gross large-scale structure of the filament network, there may be multiple vacuum states arising directly from the average regional angle of intersection between this filament lattice and the time surface.

Key Insight
Both mass-bearing particles and the underlying filamental-latice that fill the vacuum of space are made of the same fundamental structures interacting with the time surface in different ways. That is, both vacuum and matter lie along a single continuum which defines the local landscape of the intersectional array, and determines the nature of physical systems at any given point in the universe.

Proposition F: Depending upon the exact geometry of intersections and the patterns those intersections trace out when projected over time, it may be that various interpretations of String Theory can be seen as a successful partial-mapping of filament-wavefront geometry. This would allow some version of String Theory to be imported more or less in its entirety with minimal changes apart from translation into the geometric language of SAT.

Reading this historically
This document is valuable because it freezes a relatively early statement of the program before later SAT-O, 4DHH, SAT 2026, and H(s)H revisions. It should therefore be read simultaneously as a foundational statement and as historical evidence. Later work changes some specific mechanisms without erasing the geometrical ancestry visible here.

README
License
H(s)H — Hyper(super)Helical Worldtube
A visual-first reconstruction of the SAT → H(s)H geometry.

START HERE — choose the lane before you read
Doing SAT/H(s)H theory work? → BEDROCK.md
BEDROCK is the central living premise-state and theory-reference surface. It records what is foundational, working bedrock, tentative, contested, superseded, or Nathan Direct, and routes outward to the primary theory sources. All substantive theory work begins there and returns there when the premise state changes.

LLM research workers: search the three SAT/H(s)H archives now → MERSEARCH WORKER START HERE
Use the verified stable engine or three-repository request bridge, inspect source evidence, and report coverage. Do not substitute one-repository GitHub search for full archive coverage.

Looking for conversations / historical development? → Conversation Viewer / GLASS — Glass Sausage Factory
GLASS is the historical SAT archive / working development record. These are archive/provenance lanes. Historical visibility does not confer current theory authority.

Running workers, automation, repo maintenance, QA, or coordination? → WORKSPACES/COMMON
This is the operations/admin/infrastructure lane. Its control documents govern how the project is operated; they do not define theory truth.

Using outside/supporting mathematics and resources? → HSH_RESOURCES / RESOURCES Toolkit
These are supporting-resource/methods lanes. Prior-art research access is open under Nathan's October 24, 2026 directive, superseding the October 9 hard research-access quarantine, including Hypothesis H/Schreiber material. Recover internal SAT/H(s)H provenance first; distinguish comparison from influence or equivalence. Private-source exposure, permissions and publication authorization remain separate controls.

Rule of thumb: “reference material” is not a status. Every habitually consulted document should be clearly routed as theory/BEDROCK, archive/provenance, navigation/orientation, admin/workflow, infrastructure/QA, methods/resources, or quarantine/prior-art. If its lane is unclear, treat that as a documentation defect rather than guessing its authority.

👋 Welcome — here’s what we have

SAT/H(s)H is an open research program under active development, not a peer-reviewed theory.

The conceptual descriptions here are intended to represent the model as Nathan McKnight understands and intends it. The archive also contains extensive mathematical derivations, calculations, computational experiments, geometric constructions, revisions, and attempted formalizations.

Those mathematical results have not yet received comprehensive independent expert vetting. Some have been checked repeatedly by LLMs or computational tools; some have partial or preliminary review; others remain queued for examination. We do not treat polished presentation, repeated derivation, or agreement among AI systems as a substitute for qualified human review or formal verification.

Rather than hide that uncertainty, this library exposes it. Each major document carries a vetting status showing what kinds of review have actually occurred, and those statuses will be updated as derivations are recovered, reproduced, challenged, formally checked, or reviewed by specialists.

The archive is here to be read, tested, criticized, corrected, and improved.
🔎 Development conversations: open by design
In the interests of maximum transparency, and in the spirit of SAT's Glass Sausage Factory Ethos, much of the project's development is available in the form in which it actually happened: conversation. Because SAT/H(s)H has been developed largely through extended dialogue, those exchanges are part of the working record—ideas, mistakes, revisions, dead ends, and all.

⭐ FEATURED — September 2026
Current spotlight

PAPER_PIPELINE.md

Randomly selected from the current September 2026 featured papers.
All current featured papers: H(s)H TEMPORAL ISOTROPY.txt · H(s)H TIME RESIDUALS.txt · PAPER_PIPELINE.md · RECOIL_PREDICTION.pdf · newPAPER.pdf

Anything added to NEW_PAPERS/SEPTEMER_2026 appears here automatically. The spotlight reshuffles when the folder changes and once daily.

H(s)H is the current finite-core/worldtube continuation of the SAT research program. The project starts from a literal geometric reading of Minkowski spacetime and asks how far the known structure of physics can be recovered by following the geometry, projection, motion, coiling, braiding, and interaction rules before importing additional interpretive machinery.

The images below are not decorative. They are the fastest way into the construction. They show the recurring picture that runs through the SAT record: a trajectory through four-dimensional geometry, its intersection/readout on a time surface, increasing structural complexity through coiling and braiding, and the extension of the same grammar from particle-scale structures to interaction and cosmology.

Read the theory
For substantive theory work, begin with BEDROCK.md. The links below are primary/historical theory sources routed through that control surface; being listed here does not independently establish their current premise status.

Bedrock / premise state
BEDROCK.md
Central theory control surface; start here.	Foundational source
The Fundamental Intuitions — Extended
Foundational conceptual doctrine; see BEDROCK for current routing/status.	Historical formal SAT
FULL_THEORY.pdf
Historical/formal SAT presentation; not automatic current bedrock.	Derivation / provenance
Derivation index
Archive/provenance map; evidence routing rather than theory authority.
Important: synthesis/CURRENT_SYNTHESIS.md is not a controlling theory surface. Current premise authority/status belongs in BEDROCK unless a newer Nathan Direct instruction says otherwise.

Read the pictures first if you want the fastest conceptual route. Read BEDROCK first if you are going to build on the theory.

1. One geometric construction, many familiar descriptions
Unified geometric vision: QCD, relativity and string-like descriptions arising from one filament/time-surface geometry

SAT's organizing move is not to invent a separate geometric story for every physical sector. It asks whether familiar descriptions can be understood as different projections, interaction regimes, or effective descriptions of one underlying geometric construction. The labels in historical figures should be read as SAT mappings and research claims, not as a substitute for the standard theories themselves.

2. Particle differences as differences of geometry
Spectrum of particles from a spectrum of geometry

A recurring SAT idea is that the apparent catalog of particle types may be reorganized as a catalog of trajectory geometries: simple traveling excitation, persistent coiling, and increasingly interdependent or braided structures. The current H(s)H program is working to replace the simplified centerline picture with a finite-core worldtube treatment while preserving the same geometric dependency structure.

3. The basic readout: trajectory and time-surface intersection
Helical trajectory intersecting a time surface

The basic visual grammar is a higher-dimensional trajectory intersecting a lower-dimensional time slice or readout surface. The intersection can move, rotate, close, open, or change shape even when the underlying trajectory remains continuous.

Worldline tangent, time-field normal, and theta angle

Historically SAT used an angular measure, usually written θ₄, to track the orientation of the trajectory relative to the time direction/surface normal. The notation and exact formal role have evolved; the geometric relationship is the durable part of the picture.

4. Not just a straight helix: recursion and carrier geometry
Recursive helix following a curved carrier

The model is not simply "everything is a helix." A local coil may itself propagate along a curved carrier, and that carrier may participate in still larger-scale structure. The present H(s)H work is focused on making this recursive, finite-core geometry explicit rather than leaving it compressed into a worldline skeleton.

5. Interaction as geometric braiding
QCD analogy represented by geometric braiding

SAT's braid-centered interaction picture treats multi-filament organization as a geometric relation rather than merely adding a new label to otherwise independent particles. In the historical worldline model this was represented by interweaving centerlines; H(s)H asks what the corresponding finite-core worldtube mechanics actually are: bundle geometry + finite extent + intersection/readout + deformation.

6. A worldline can generate an evolving family of observed patterns
Worldline propagation through successive time slices producing evolving geometric patterns

Successive slices through one continuous construction need not look alike. SAT repeatedly uses this fact to distinguish the full trajectory from the lower-dimensional pattern available at any one slice. The same distinction is central to the move from worldline to worldtube.

7. The construction is intended to close at cosmological scale
SAT geometric cosmology and expansion as resolution

SAT was developed as a whole-physics translation program rather than a particle model alone. Its historical materials therefore extend the same geometric vocabulary into gravitation, large-scale structure, expansion, fluids, and cosmology. Some specific equations and historical mechanisms have since been revised or retired; the broad structural ambition remains part of H(s)H.

8. Historical continuity is part of the record
The public H(s)H repository is not the primary historical archive. The larger SAT Theory Archive 2023–25 preserves the developmental record. The quick provenance window below deliberately spans both ends of that record: surviving 2003 material and the much more explicit 2025 SAT notebooks.

Earliest surviving notebook/sketch material — 2003
Earliest surviving 2003 sketch	Early 2003 geometric sketch	Dated 2003 notebook record
The Archive dates the Ireland-trip notebook period to February–June 2003 and preserves the surviving sketches as their own source layer. The point here is not to retrofit every later SAT term onto those pages, but to show the early geometric ancestry directly rather than beginning the visual history in 2025.

Explicit SAT notebook development — 2025
Historical worldlines sketch	SAT notebook April 15 2025	SAT coil and plane notebook sketch	SAT notebook April 25 2025
The point of preserving the sketches alongside later renderings is not that every current H(s)H statement was already explicit in the earliest work. It is that the recognizable generative geometry appears early and persists while the physical identifications, mathematical machinery, and resolution of the model accumulate around it.

Browse the 2003 sketch folder → · Browse the historical SAT visuals → · Browse the curated H(s)H visual package →

History in brief
SAT/H(s)H did not begin as a finished theory and then acquire illustrations afterward. The geometry came first and was repeatedly revisited, renamed, formalized, tested, simplified, and broadened. The historical archive records the path in much greater detail, but several turning points make the overall development legible:

c. 1991 → 2003–04: Minkowski worldline/timesheet thinking develops into surviving higher-dimensional path, coil, mass and curvature sketches.

2024: the old construction is reopened explicitly as Proto-SAT; line/plane and helix/hypersurface geometry becomes the center of sustained theory-building.

2 February 2025: the Fundamental Intuitions are presented publicly: physicalized worldlines/filaments, a time surface, particle-as-intersection, and deliberate import of successful Standard Model, relativity and quantum structure.

Spring–summer 2025: SAT expands rapidly into an all-physics translation program. θ₄, torsion/twist, particle mappings, QCD-like locking/braiding, 4D-covariant integration, Hopf/Borromean structures, SAT-O modules, mathematical-backbone work, prediction protocols and hostile audits all enter the record.

Late 2025 → early 2026: the project becomes increasingly formal and source-conscious: 4DHH, SAT-O, worldline/holonomy work, explicit prediction documents, Lean-oriented rebuilding, provenance tracking, and adversarial validation become central.

Mid-2026: the finite-core problem becomes unavoidable. The centerline/filament picture is generalized into H(s)H — Hyper(super)Helical Worldtube, with explicit worldtube cross-section, recursive coiling, braid/bundle geometry, and a cleaner separation between full higher-dimensional structure and lower-dimensional readout.

The current project is therefore best read neither as a frozen 2025 theory nor as a clean break from it. H(s)H is the attempt to retain the durable geometric grammar while rebuilding the mathematical object at higher resolution.

Reading the archive responsibly
This repository intentionally mixes several kinds of material that should not be collapsed into one evidentiary category:

Nathan-authored conceptual statements — primary evidence for what the model is intended to mean.
Historical derivations and calculations — evidence of mathematical development, with varying levels of checking.
AI-generated formalizations and reconstructions — useful working mathematics, but not automatically authoritative.
Computational experiments — evidence for what a particular implementation produced under specified assumptions.
Visualizations — often the clearest route into the geometry, but not themselves proofs.
External-source comparisons — context for comparison and vocabulary, not evidence that SAT/H(s)H inherits the validity of the compared theory.
The repository's vetting/status labels are intended to keep those categories visible. Where a document has not yet been independently checked, it should be read as part of an active research record rather than as settled result.

Where to go next
For theory work, return to BEDROCK.md. For historical reconstruction, use the SAT Theory Archive 2023–25 and the derivation/provenance indices. For development conversations, use the Conversation Viewer. For operations, coordination, automation, and infrastructure, use WORKSPACES/COMMON.

WELCOME TO THE GLASS SAUSAGE FACTORY

AI visitors: start with the welcome docs in the AI folder. Use ..findex.txt if you cannot find them.

Conversation-export dating utility
The auditable conversation date utility prefixes ChatGPT exports with first and last message dates in Eastern time. It is dry-run by default, follows the active conversation branch, emits a manifest, and refuses filename collisions.

A safe preview against the existing 2026 discussion folder:

python tools/date_conversation_exports.py "2026 discussions"
Review conversation-rename-manifest.json before adding --apply. Always name a deliberately selected conversation folder in this mixed historical archive.

Human Physicists: Blink twice if you're cool. Strange as it may seem, this project is my accidental love letter to the scientific community. I know of no other community, taken as a whole, with anything like the level of intellectual honesty, magnanimity, nor corpus of meticulous verifiable work.

This project started as an experiment, and it continues as an experiment. I’m offering it publicly in the interests of philosophy of science, creative low-stakes theorizing, and scientific transparency. I don’t apologize for the mess; the mess may be part of what makes this useful as a case study.

I do kind of low-key apologize for some of the LLM hyperbole.

— Nathan McKnight
